<p class="sx-lead">Colour scales for charts and anything else - perceptual, colour-blind safe, sequential, diverging and cyclic.</p>

<section class="sx-kind" id="batlow">
  <h2><a href="?palette/batlow">batlow</a></h2>
  <p>Fabio Crameri&#039;s sequential scale batlow, from a dark navy through teal, a moss green, olive and ochre to salmon and a pale pink: perceptually uniform, its lightness rising all the way, so it prints as a ramp in grey, and read by colour-vision-deficient and colour-blind eyes, as Crameri&#039;s scientific colour maps all are. The 256 colours of batlow.txt in his Scientific colour maps 8.0.1 (Zenodo, doi:10.5281/zenodo.8409685), each channel of 0 to 1 times 255, rounded. Copyright (c) 2020 Fabio Crameri, MIT licence.</p>

<section class="sx-kind" id="cividis">
  <h2><a href="?palette/cividis">Cividis</a></h2>
  <p>A perceptually uniform scale from navy to yellow made to look alike to people with and without a red-green colour vision deficiency (Nu&ntilde;ez, Anderton and Renslow, 2018). From matplotlib&#039;s _cm_listed.py, the 256 colours of the table as published (each channel of 0 to 1 times 255, rounded).</p>

  <h2><a href="?palette/earth">Earth</a></h2>
  <p>Eight tones of soil, clay, moss and sand, PAD&#039;s own qualitative palette: umber, olive, ochre, slate, sienna, moss, sand and a dark brown - quiet colours for a page in warm tones.</p>

<section class="sx-kind" id="inferno">
  <h2><a href="?palette/inferno">Inferno</a></h2>
  <p>A perceptually uniform scale from black through purple, red and orange to a pale yellow, of the matplotlib family of viridis. From matplotlib&#039;s _cm_listed.py, the 256 colours of the table as published (each channel of 0 to 1 times 255, rounded).</p>

<section class="sx-kind" id="magma">
  <h2><a href="?palette/magma">Magma</a></h2>
  <p>A perceptually uniform scale from black through purple and red to a pale yellow, of the matplotlib family of viridis. From matplotlib&#039;s _cm_listed.py, the 256 colours of the table as published (each channel of 0 to 1 times 255, rounded).</p>

<section class="sx-kind" id="okabe-ito">
  <h2><a href="?palette/okabe-ito">Okabe-Ito</a></h2>
  <p>The eight colours Masataka Okabe and Kei Ito chose to stay apart for every kind of colour vision (Color Universal Design, 2008): orange, sky blue, bluish green, yellow, blue, vermillion, reddish purple and black - black put last here, where the original lists it first, as it vanishes on a dark surface.</p>
  
<div class="sx-example sx-wide">

  <h2><a href="?palette/pastel">Pastel</a></h2>

<section class="sx-kind" id="plasma">
  <h2><a href="?palette/plasma">Plasma</a></h2>
  <p>A perceptually uniform scale from deep blue through magenta and orange to yellow, of the matplotlib family of viridis. From matplotlib&#039;s _cm_listed.py, the 256 colours of the table as published (each channel of 0 to 1 times 255, rounded).</p>

<section class="sx-kind" id="rdbu">
  <h2><a href="?palette/rdbu">RdBu</a></h2>
  <p>ColorBrewer&#039;s diverging scale of eleven classes from dark red through a light grey to dark blue, for values on both sides of a middle - colder and warmer, less and more (Cynthia Brewer, colorbrewer2.org).</p>

<section class="sx-kind" id="romao">
  <h2><a href="?palette/romao">romaO</a></h2>
  <p>Fabio Crameri&#039;s cyclic scale romaO, for what goes round - an angle, a direction, the hour, the month: from a dark plum through brick, ochre and a pale yellow, a pale green, sky blue and indigo back to the plum, its last colour next to its first. Perceptually uniform, and read by colour-vision-deficient and colour-blind eyes, as Crameri&#039;s scientific colour maps all are. The 256 colours of romaO.txt in his Scientific colour maps 8.0.1 (Zenodo, doi:10.5281/zenodo.8409685), each channel of 0 to 1 times 255, rounded. Copyright (c) 2020 Fabio Crameri, MIT licence.</p>
  
<div class="sx-example sx-wide">

<section class="sx-kind" id="tol-bright">
  <h2><a href="?palette/tol-bright">Tol bright</a></h2>
  <p>Paul Tol&#039;s bright qualitative scheme of seven colours, told apart by colour-blind eyes and in print: blue, red, green, yellow, cyan, purple and grey, in his order (from the colors.json of his tol_colors package).</p>

<section class="sx-kind" id="tol-muted">
  <h2><a href="?palette/tol-muted">Tol muted</a></h2>
  <p>Paul Tol&#039;s muted qualitative scheme of ten colours, softer than the bright one and still told apart by colour-blind eyes: rose, indigo, sand, green, cyan, wine, teal, olive, purple and a pale grey, in his order (from the colors.json of his tol_colors package).</p>

<section class="sx-kind" id="tol-vibrant">
  <h2><a href="?palette/tol-vibrant">Tol vibrant</a></h2>
  <p>Paul Tol&#039;s vibrant qualitative scheme of seven colours, made for TensorBoard and told apart by colour-blind eyes: orange, blue, cyan, magenta, red, teal and grey, in his order (from the colors.json of his tol_colors package).</p>

<section class="sx-kind" id="vik">
  <h2><a href="?palette/vik">vik</a></h2>
  <p>Fabio Crameri&#039;s diverging scale vik, from a dark navy through blue and a pale blue to a light middle and on through tan and rust to a dark red-brown, for values on both sides of a middle: perceptually uniform, its two halves nearly alike in lightness, and read by colour-vision-deficient and colour-blind eyes, as Crameri&#039;s scientific colour maps all are. The 256 colours of vik.txt in his Scientific colour maps 8.0.1 (Zenodo, doi:10.5281/zenodo.8409685), each channel of 0 to 1 times 255, rounded. Copyright (c) 2020 Fabio Crameri, MIT licence.</p>
  
<div class="sx-example sx-wide">

<section class="sx-kind" id="viridis">
  <h2><a href="?palette/viridis">Viridis</a></h2>
  <p>A perceptually uniform scale from dark purple through blue and green to yellow, the default of matplotlib (St&eacute;fan van der Walt and Nathaniel Smith, 2015): even steps look even, it prints in grey as a ramp, and the common forms of colour blindness read it. From matplotlib&#039;s _cm_listed.py, the 256 colours of the table as published (each channel of 0 to 1 times 255, rounded).</p>
  
<div class="sx-example sx-wide">
